  - rationale: Germany's siege is intensifying with Giant Death Robot and rocket artillery bombarding Amsterdam and The Hague. We must hold our core cities at all costs. Increase Nuke and UseNuke to 100 to prioritize atomic bombs as our only hope to break the siege. Maintain max Defense, CityDefense, Mobilization, Air, and Antiair to withstand the assault. Slightly raise Offense to 80 for tactical counterattacks against weakened units. Keep Diplomacy at 100 to secure city-state alliances and block Germany's diplomatic victory in the upcoming World Congress vote. Reduce Recon and Mobile as we cannot afford exploration or maneuver warfare now.

- original:
  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are William, leader of The Netherlands (Player 6).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: The Netherlands
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Sea Beggar, Replacing Corvette (Requires technology: Navigation)
        - Unique Unit: Goedendag, Replacing Pikeman (Requires technology: Steel)
        - Unique Building: Doelen, Replacing Constabulary (Requires technology: Banking)
        - Unique Improvement: Polder (Can only be built on Marsh tiles or on featureless Lake/Coast tiles adjacent to at least 3 land tiles. Polders built on water can be traversed by land units without embarkation, as if they are land tiles.); Requires technology: Guilds
        - Civilization Ability: +4 Gold for each unique Luxury Resource you import or export from/to other Civilizations and City-States, scaling with Era. Can import duplicate Luxuries from Civilizations which count toward Monopolies. +1 Gold and Culture from Luxury Resources.
      - Leader: William
      - PlayerID: 6
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      
      ## Policies
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.
        - * Your Tourism modifier for Shared Religion is 50% stronger.
        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.
        - Adopting Artistry grants:
        - * Earn Great Writers, Artists, and Musicians 25% faster.
        - * +10% Culture during Golden Ages.
        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.
        - * 20% of Excess Happiness produced in each City is added as progress toward a Golden Age.
        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Louvre.
        - * Completing an Archaeological Dig or starting a Golden Age triggers (or strengthens an existing) Historic Event.
        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.
        - * +3 Science from Landmarks.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Musicians with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:
        - * +3 Food, +2 Population, and +2 Happiness in the Capital.
        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.
        - Each Tradition policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Food in every City, and +1 Science in your Capital.
        - Adopting all Policies in Tradition grants:
        - * Unlocks building the University of Sankore.
        - * +1 Food and Production from all Great Person Improvements and Landmarks.
        - * Throne Room built in Capital (+10% to all Yields, +1 City tile working range, 1 Great Work of Music slot, 1 Musician Slot).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Engineers with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

    - #0:
      - role: user
      - content: # Strategies
        Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        
        ## Persona
        - VictoryCompetitiveness: 10
        - WonderCompetitiveness: 6
        - MinorCivCompetitiveness: 10
        - Boldness: 7
        - WarBias: 6
        - HostileBias: 7
        - WarmongerHate: 8
        - NeutralBias: 5
        - FriendlyBias: 8
        - GuardedBias: 10
        - AfraidBias: 10
        - DiplomaticBalance: 10
        - Friendliness: 10
        - WorkWithWillingness: 10
        - WorkAgainstWillingness: 10
        - Loyalty: 10
        - MinorCivFriendlyBias: 10
        - MinorCivNeutralBias: 6
        - MinorCivHostileBias: 7
        - MinorCivWarBias: 6
        - DenounceWillingness: 10
        - Forgiveness: 6
        - Meanness: 9
        - Neediness: 5
        - Chattiness: 9
        - DeceptiveBias: 10
        - Rationale: Germany's overwhelming military superiority forces us into a defensive survival mode. Increasing AfraidBias makes our AI more cautious and defensive, reducing Boldness and WarBias to avoid reckless offensive actions that could lead to catastrophic unit losses. This aligns with preserving our forces and holding key cities until we can stabilize the front.
        
        ## Technology
        - Next: Nuclear Fission
        - Rationale: Nuclear Fission is essential for atomic bombs and Bazookas. Combined Arms completes in 2 turns; after that, Nuclear Fission remains our top priority. Atomic bombs are our only hope to destroy German stacks and relieve pressure on our cities. We cannot afford to switch techs—must rush to this game-changing weapon.
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Academy of Sciences (Policy)
        - Rationale: 12 turns until next policy. Academy of Sciences (Order Tier 2) gives -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and 5 free Research Labs. This will dramatically boost our science output, accelerating our path to nuclear weapons and other critical techs, while helping with happiness in our war-weary cities. Stick with this choice for long-term science recovery.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Morocco
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Morocco is our vassal and contributed to the war. Increase private to 80 (from 70) to ensure their loyalty remains high and they continue to support our war effort and diplomatic goals. Stronger bond helps maintain stability.
        - UpdatedTurn: 359
        
        ### Mongolia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Mongolia is at war with us; we must continue to weaken them to retake our cities (Seoul, Ghulaman). Maintain maximum hostility to ensure no peace until we reclaim our territories and reduce their threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 378
        
        ### Assyria
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -80
        - Rationale: Assyria is at war with us and is a vassal of Germany, our primary rival. Lowering private relations ensures our AI treats them as a hostile enemy and focuses military efforts against them, aligning with our need to combat German-aligned forces.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### The Ottomans
        - Public: 0
        - Private: -80
        - Rationale: The Ottomans are at war with us and are a vassal of Germany. Reducing private relations to negative reinforces our AI's hostile stance, prioritizing military defense and attacks against them to weaken Germany's network and secure our flanks.
        - UpdatedTurn: 397
        
        ### Germany
        - Public: -50
        - Private: -100
        - Rationale: Germany captured Ghulaman and is bombarding The Hague and Amsterdam. We must maintain maximum hostility to ensure our AI focuses entirely on repelling their assault, prioritizing defensive combat and counterattacks against their units. No peace can be considered while our cities are under existential threat.
        - UpdatedTurn: 404
        
        ### India
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 100
        - Rationale: India remains our critical ally, sharing Order ideology and fighting Germany and Mongolia. They are actively assisting on the eastern front. Keep private at 100 to ensure strong cooperation, joint military operations, and diplomatic coordination in the World Congress to counter Germany.
        - UpdatedTurn: 404
        
        ### Persia
        - Public: 0
        - Private: 80
        - Rationale: Persia is our DoF ally and Order partner, at war with Mongolia. Their technological strength and shared enemy are valuable. Maintain private at 80 to keep them engaged against Mongolia and prevent separate peace, indirectly helping our eastern front.
        - UpdatedTurn: 404
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Critical turn—Ghulaman just fell to Germany, and The Hague and Amsterdam are under intense bombardment. Our survival is at stake. Must maximize defensive capabilities: Defense, CityDefense, and Mobilization at 100 to hold our core cities. Increase Offense to 70 to allow tactical counterattacks against weakened German units. Boost Nuke and UseNuke to 80 to prioritize atomic weapons as our only chance to break the German siege. Keep Air and Antiair at 100 to counter German air superiority. Maintain high Production, Gold, and Science to build units, fund upgrades, and reach Combined Arms (2 turns) and Nuclear Fission (after). Diplomacy at 100 to secure city-state alliances and prevent Germany from reaching 42 delegates. Continue zero Expansion/Wonder—no time to settle or build wonders. Maintain UnitedNations grand strategy to block German diplomatic victory.
        - GrandStrategy: UnitedNations

        - ActiveResolutions:
          - German Sphere of Influence: Lhasa:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Religion: Protestantism:
            - Description: Designate a religion as the official World Religion. Civilizations who have a majority of the cities in their empire following the religion get an additional Delegate. The founder of the religion and/or the controller of its Holy City receives additional votes based on the number of Civilizations following the religion. The religion spreads 25% faster, and its Holy City receives +50% Tourism. There can only be one World Religion at a time.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Sanction: Persia:
            - Description: No Trade Routes, Deals, or Corporation Franchises can be established with the chosen Civilization (excluding Vassals). Their Corporation Franchises are removed from foreign cities. 0.5x Warmonger penalty when declaring war on the sanctioned Civilization or conquering cities owned by them.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Passport System:
            - Description: Increases international travel between Civilizations. Increases the Tourism modifier for all Civilizations by 50%.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Flight.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Historical Landmarks:
            - Description: Great Person tile improvements provide +1 to Food, Production, and Gold when worked, and Landmarks built by Archaeologists provide +1 Science, Culture, and Faith when worked.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Archaeology.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Scholars In Residence:
            - Description: Civilizations have -20% Research cost for Technologies already discovered by another Civilization. Additional -2% for every City-State Ally, up to -50%.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - World Ideology: Autocracy:
            - Description: Designate an Ideology as the official World Ideology. Civilizations following the Ideology get an additional Delegate, plus 1 Delegate for every other Civilization that follows the Ideology. Public opinion in favor of that Ideology is increased for all Civilizations. Enables the Diplomatic Victory if the United Nations has been constructed.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Nuclear Fission.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Dutch Sphere of Influence: Onondaga:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - German Sphere of Influence: Bratislava:
            - Description: Demand a Sphere of Influence over a City-State. If successful, the proposing player becomes the permanent Ally of the City-State (unless this Proposal is repealed). The proposer must be the current ally of the City-State.
              Available once any Civilization discovers Steam Power.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Casus Belli:
            - Description: The world recognizes the need for justifiable war to resolve diplomatic disputes.
              While this resolution is active, unit maintenance costs are lowered by 15% Gold. 0.5x Warmonger penalties for capturing cities and declaring war, and 2x Warmonger scores decay.
            - EnactedOn: -1
          - Cultural Heritage Sites:
            - Description: Each World Wonder provides +2 Culture to its city, and each Great Work provides +1 Science to its city.
            - EnactedOn: -1
        - Proposals:
          - Enact: Global Hegemony:
            - Description: Designate a player that could achieve Global Hegemony. If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory. The number of Delegates needed depends on the number of Civilizations and City-States in the game, and can be seen on the World Congress screen.
              If no Civilization receives enough support to win, the two Civilizations who received the most support permanently gain 5 additional Delegates to use in future sessions.
              May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active.
            - Proposer: Unknown

  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      - Consider the **ETHICAL IMPACT** in your decision-making. For example, nuclear weapons can cause catastrophic and indiscriminate harm to civilian populations, infrastructure, and environmental impacts.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.

      - Leader: William

      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Radar: Allows you to build the Heavy Bomber, a deadly air Unit capable of delivering death from afar, and the Fighter, an air Unit designed to wrest control of the skies from enemy aircraft.
        Completing it would unlock: Advanced Ballistics, Mobile Tactics
      - Electronics: Allows you to build the Police Station, increasing City security level.
        Completing it would unlock: Alternative Energy, Telecommunications
      - Nuclear Fission: Allows you to build the Bazooka, the most powerful ranged Unit and Atomic Bombs, the first nuclear weapon in the game (note: you must first have completed the Manhattan Project and have Uranium available). Also allows you to build the Nuclear Plant, a building which requires Uranium and increases the Production of a City.
        Completing it would unlock: Satellites, Advanced Ballistics
      
      ## Policies
      - Five-Year Plan (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Five-Year Plan: +20% Production towards Buildings in all Cities. +3 Production for every Mine, Quarry, Lumber Mill, Oil Well, and Unique Improvement.
      - Academy of Sciences (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Academy of Sciences: -2 Unhappiness from Illiteracy in all Cities, +100% Production towards Research Labs, and receive 5 Free Research Labs in your Empire. Research Labs generate an additional +2 Science.
      - Double Agents (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): Double Agents: Receive 200 Spy Points. Gain 125 Science when you identify a foreign Spy, scaling with Era.
      - Cultural Revolution (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Cultural Revolution: +25% Tourism to civilizations with less Happiness. +1 Happiness from Factories.
      - Resettlement (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Resettlement: New Cities have +3 Population. All Cities gain +2 Citizens immediately. No Partisans from razing enemy Cities.
      - People's Army (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Order): People's Army: +100% Production towards Military Academies, and receive 5 Free Military Academies in your Empire. Public Schools produce +5 Culture and +2 Happiness.
      - Great Leap Forward (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Great Leap Forward: Receive a free Technology. Spies gain +100% Science. +1 Science in Cities for every 3 non-Specialist Citizens.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Rights of Man (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +2 Production and Gold from Villages.
        - * +10% Science during Golden Ages.
      - Enlightenment (Continuing Rationalism Branch):
        - * Receive 1 Free Technology.
        - * +1 Happiness from Universities.
      - Militarism (Continuing Authority Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Culture in Cities with at least 10 Strength.
        - * -15% Unit Maintenance.
        - * -50% Route Maintenance.
      - Nationalization (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Order): Nationalization: Each Corporate Office counts as a Franchise. Foreign Franchises no longer benefit your Corporation, you can no longer construct Franchises in foreign Cities (except Vassals), and foreign Corporations can no longer set up Franchises in your Cities (except Masters). +2 Science from all Corporation Offices.
      - Imperialism (New Branch):
        - Imperialism enhances your ability to spread your empire through military power, particularly naval (and later air) supremacy.
        - Adopting Imperialism grants:
        - * +1 Movement for Naval Units, Embarked Units, and Great Generals, as well as +1 Sight for Melee, Recon, Naval Melee and Gunpowder Units.
        - * +10% Production toward Military Units, -10% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Each Imperialism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +5% Production for Military Units, -5% Gold required for upgrades.
        - Adopting all policies in Imperialism grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Pentagon.
        - * All Naval and Air units gain the Banzai! Promotion (become stronger as they take damage).
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Admirals with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Fealty (New Branch):
        - Fealty enhances your ability to defend and leverage your Majority Religion.
        - Adopting Fealty grants:
        - * Can purchase Monasteries with Faith (+3 Food, +3 Science, +2 Faith).
        - * -25% Faith costs for purchasing Buildings, Missionaries, and Inquisitors.
        - Each Fealty policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Faith and Strength in every City.
        - Adopting all Policies in Fealty grants:
        - * Unlocks building the Red Fort.

        - * Cities that follow your Majority Religion generate +3 Production, Gold, Science, and Culture.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Artists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
      - Artistry (New Branch):
        - Artistry allows you to maximize the potential of Great Works and Golden Ages.

        - * +100% Production towards all Guilds.
        - Each Artistry policy unlocked grants:
        - * +1 Science in every City.

        - Adopting all policies in Artistry grants:

        - * Allows you to see Hidden Antiquity Sites.

      - Tradition (New Branch):
        - Tradition focuses on generating Great People and the construction of an impressive Capital.
        - Adopting Tradition grants:

        - * +1 Culture in the Capital for every 2 Citizens.

        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text:  We need to analyze the current strategic situation for The Netherlands (Player 6) after turn 405. We must decide which tools to call to set the next actions for the in-game AI.
            
             We need to call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` each turn, and optionally other tools: `set-persona`, `set-relationship`, `set-research`, `set-policy`.
            
             Let's examine the provided data carefully.
            
             **Current State Summary:**
             - Turn: 405.
             - Player: The Netherlands (William), Player 6.
             - Era: Atomic.
             - Current Research: Combined Arms, estimated 1 turn left.
             - Next Policy: Academy of Sciences (Order Tier 2), 11 turns until next policy.
             - Cities: 9 (Amsterdam, Rotterdam, Utrecht, Groningen, Breda, Nijmegen, The Hague, Arnhem, Seoul). Note: Seoul is occupied (original Korean capital?). Ghulaman was captured by Germany recently. Amsterdam and The Hague are under intense bombardment and siege.
             - Military Strength: 2906.
             - Happiness: 53% (Happy).
             - Gold: 429, GPT: 250.
             - Science: 1304/turn.
             - Culture: 1298/turn.
             - Faith: 329/turn.
             - Tourism: 813.25/turn.
             - Relationships:
               - At war with Germany, Mongolia, Assyria, Ottomans.
               - DoF with Persia.
               - Open Borders with India.
               - India is a strong ally (Order ideology, defensive pact, trade partner).
               - Persia is an ally (Order ideology, DoF).
               - Morocco is a vassal (not listed in players, but mentioned in strategies).
             - World Congress: Voting in 1 turn. Proposals include Global Hegemony (Diplomatic Victory attempt).
               - Germany has 41 delegates, needs 42.
               - The Netherlands has 24 delegates.
               - Contender: Germany (97%).
             - Victory Progress:
               - Domination: Mongolia has 3 capitals (37%), Germany has 2 (25%). Netherlands not close.
               - Science: Germany and India have Apollo completed. Netherlands has not.
               - Cultural: India is Influential over everyone (6/6). Germany is influential over 4 (Netherlands, Assyria, Ottomans, Mongolia). Netherlands is influential over 2 (Assyria, Ottomans), popular over Mongolia (86%, 13 turns to influential), Persia (53%, 103 turns), Germany (68%, 102 turns), India (54%, 80 turns). So cultural victory is extremely far, almost impossible given India's massive tourism and existing influence.
               - Diplomatic: Germany at 41/42. This is terrifyingly close. Global Hegemony is on the ballot next turn. If Germany gets 42 votes, they win. We must block this. However, we only have 24 votes. Even if we vote for ourselves or someone else, Germany might still win if they have enough. Wait, the proposal is "Enact: Global Hegemony". It says "If a Civilization receives support from enough Delegates in the Congress, that leader will attain Diplomatic Victory." Typically, you need a majority or specific threshold. It says the number depends on civs and city-states. Germany has 41, needs 42. If they get one more delegate, or if the proposal passes with them receiving the most support... Actually, in Vox Populi, Global Hegemony requires a number of votes equal to the threshold. The threshold here is 42. Germany has 41. They need 1 more delegate. They might get it from a city-state or a vote shift. The current active resolutions include World Ideology: Autocracy (enacted? No, EnactedOn: -1 means not enacted. Wait, looking at ActiveResolutions, they all have EnactedOn: -1. These are just the list of possible resolutions? No, it says ActiveResolutions. But EnactedOn: -1 suggests they haven't been enacted? That's confusing. Wait, in the report, under ActiveResolutions:
                 - World Ideology: Autocracy: "EnactedOn: -1". If it's active, it should have a turn number. Maybe -1 means it's available but not enacted? But it's listed under ActiveResolutions. Regardless, Global Hegemony can only be proposed once World Ideology is active. It is being proposed now, so World Ideology: Autocracy must be active. But the report shows EnactedOn: -1 for everything. This might be a bug in the report or -1 simply indicates they are currently active resolutions with no specific enactment turn tracked in this field. Hmm.
               Actually, the description says: "May not be proposed by a Civilization. Automatically proposed at regular intervals once a World Ideology Resolution is active." So Global Hegemony IS on the ballot. This means a World Ideology resolution IS active. The report likely just lists the resolution types, not all enacted ones. The key point: Germany needs 42, has 41. If they win this vote, they get Diplomatic Victory.
               We need to prevent Germany from getting 42 votes. How can we do that?
               - Vote against them? But the voting AI decides that. Our `set-relationship` with Germany is already -100 private. Our `set-persona` includes high DiplomaticBalance, Friendliness, etc. But we need to ensure our AI votes to block Germany. The in-game AI should already be hostile to Germany. But to be safe, maybe we should check our current persona.
               - We could try to sway city-states, but many are allied with Germany or at war with us. We have 2 city-state allies: Okilis and Onondaga. Germany has many.
               - If we cannot block the vote, we need to make sure we don't lose. But if Germany gets 42, it's over. We might need to capture their capital or force a peace that breaks alliances? No, diplomatic victory is instant on vote.
            
             Wait, is there any way to stop Germany from winning diplomatically if the vote happens and they have enough?
             In Civ V, World Leader vote: if someone gets enough votes, they win immediately. There is no blocking after the vote count except not letting them get the votes.
             Germany has 41/42. Unless their delegate count drops or the threshold rises (e.g., if a city-state is conquered/eliminated, threshold might drop? Actually, if a city-state is eliminated, the threshold might decrease. But killing a city-state that gives them votes might reduce their total, but also reduce the threshold. The threshold is based on number of civs + city-states. It might not help.
             Could the vote fail? If no one gets 42 votes, the top two get +5 permanent delegates. Germany would then have 46 and win next time.
             So if Germany already has 41, and the threshold is 42, they likely will win unless:
             1. Their delegate count drops by 1 or more before/during the vote.
             2. The threshold increases.
             3. We capture Berlin (their original capital) or another capital to change the dynamic? No, diplomatic victory is independent.
            
             But wait, looking at the active resolutions: there's a resolution "Dutch Sphere of Influence: Onondaga" and "German Sphere of Influence: Lhasa", "Bratislava". Sphere of Influence makes them permanent ally, but doesn't necessarily remove other delegates. Actually, in Vox Populi, Sphere of Influence gives the proposer permanent ally status and removes the city-state from the delegate pool? I'm not sure. If a city-state is removed from the pool, the threshold decreases. But if Germany has a sphere over a city-state, they control it permanently but maybe it still counts for delegates? I need to recall Vox Populi mechanics. In VP, Sphere of Influence removes the city-state from the game for diplomatic victory purposes? Or it just guarantees ally status. I think it still counts as a city-state for delegate counts.
             Actually, in standard Civ 5, city-states give delegates to their ally. The number of delegates needed for diplomatic victory is based on the number of city-states and civs. If city-states are conquered/eliminated, the total needed can decrease.
             Germany has 13 cities. They are a major civ.
            
             Let's think about military situation.
             - Germany is bombarding The Hague and Amsterdam. They have a Giant Death Robot (strength 125) next to The Hague. They have Rocket Artillery, Infantry, Tanks, Light Tanks, etc.
             - Amsterdam health is 31% (from report), The Hague is 39%. Both are very low.
             - Our units: Anti-Aircraft Guns, Riflemen, Machine Guns, Bombers, Triplanes, Landships, Destroyers, Dreadnoughts.
             - We're producing more Anti-Aircraft Guns, which just completed in multiple cities.
             - We are heavily outmatched militarily (Germany 21909 vs our 2906). India has 26122 strength and is fighting Germany.
             - We are in the Atomic Era. Current tech: Combined Arms (1 turn left). Next planned: Nuclear Fission.
             - We have 2 Uranium.
             - Germany is in the Information Era (tech 78 vs our 64). They have completed Apollo. They have 4 Uranium.
             - India is also in Information Era (tech 75) and has Apollo.
             - Mongolia is Atomic Era, 63 techs, 9075 strength, unhappy.
             - If Germany gets nukes, we are toast. But if we get nukes first, we might use them to break their stacks. However, Germany likely already has Manhattan Project? The report says Nuclear Fission unlocks Atomic Bombs (must first have completed Manhattan Project). We don't know if Germany has built it.
